A Table Without a Master","volume":"婚后外传：公爵烧坏了午饭","anchor":"chapter-0195","paragraphs":["The final basket contained enough fruit for a small orchard and a single instruction: make something to share. Cassian looked mournfully at the absence of steak. \"At last,\" Mara said, \"a round in which meat cannot betray you.\"","\"I have been loyal to it.\"","\"The loyalty has been one-sided.\"","He stole a kiss while she was trying to open the melon. His improvement in the kitchen was undoubted; his sense of timing remained incurably opportunistic. She gave him the spoon and appointed him keeper of the juice.","Across the aisle, Flint ordered Ess to recreate a complicated dessert from memory. She told him they lacked the ingredients. He made a sarcastic remark about imagination. This time she answered that imagination would not put missing food on the table. She had made a potato pie for the crew too, using the last dough. Her voice was quiet, and the people nearest her heard every word. She was tired of being treated as a low-wage extension of his hands.","Mara did not need to liken the exchange to a battle. Nobody should have to win a war to describe an empty cupboard. She asked the host whether the final round allowed assistants to enter independently. The stated rules allowed any registered cook aboard to compete. Ess had registered, had cooked and had served the passengers. Her role on Flint's team did not erase that.","Flint objected. A junior cook’s preference was of small account to him. The judges let Ess decide. She wanted to finish the dessert she had already planned, using the supplies allotted to her station. She would not take his prize whisky or help herself to the bar's whiskey. She did need the reserved fruit and a clean bowl. Mara handed her the spare one from their table. The contestants opposite each other were smiling now.","The pudding would need a crisp finish. Cassian's duty was to make the fruit topping. He cut a strawberry too fiercely and sent it skittering toward the rail. Mara caught it, discarded the dropped piece and put another in his hand. \"An intuitive lesson in knife pressure,\" she said.","\"I insist on doing the next one with dignity.\"","A small pre-teen audience applauded his successful slice. Remy held his mice in their secure box on the passenger deck, far from the food. He had decided they would be judges of cardboard tube quality instead. One mouse was still trying to chew its way through the same roll. Its persistence made Cassian feel less alone.","Mara assembled a simple pudding with fruit, leaving the brittle sugar decoration to Cassian. A guest whose diet excluded dairy received a different fruit portion; the dietary note stayed with the tray. Their bread base came from a leftover loaf; the kitchen had kept it properly for this use. Flint's elaborate trifle required time the competition did not offer. Ess's smaller version, made with the available ingredients, was almost ready. She had learned to reconcile an idea with the conditions actually in front of her.","At another table, a sour apple made a child pull a face; his brother offered him a grape. Their mother said chewing gum could wait until after lunch. The lunch passengers stayed to watch. The boy who had advised Cassian about the hamburger now carried a piece of bacon on his plate and a profound confidence in his own culinary judgement. The bar steward declined more brandy, choosing juice instead. His mother offered him an apple before he requested another sausage. A nearby diner preferred the mutton, while her partner asked whether more pork remained. A cloudy sky brightened above the fields of hay. The crew kept the serving trays moving so each guest could choose without crowding the contestants.","Mara took a spoonful of the warm pea side dish between tasks. Hunger had made pretty much everything smell wonderful, including the barbecue drifting up from the lower deck. Cassian brought her a small plate and made her eat while he cleaned their station. Tenderness could look like that too: no speech, just food arriving before she remembered to ask for it.","The chocolate on the winning dessert was made of the donated cocoa mixture, carefully labelled. At the judges' table, Ess's dessert won. Mara came second by a narrow margin. Cassian declared the outcome wholly satisfactory and poured her water as though presenting a priceless wine. The bar offered beer and a celebratory cocktail for those who wanted them. Ess chose a soft drink, still visibly stunned by the whopping applause.","Flint tried to make a joke of the result. Ess declined to laugh on that account. She thanked him for the skills she had learned, then said she would seek a different kitchen. She would not impoverish herself by walking out without considering rent; she would ask friends about work and read any new offer carefully. The hope was practical, not a fairy godmother turning a certificate into a livelihood.","The patriotic guest returned wearing a paper crown made by Remy. He toasted the boat, the crew and the mice in an order nobody fully approved. Ess laughed so freely that even Flint looked embarrassed by his earlier performance. Thereby the afternoon acquired a better ending than a staged apology. She had stopped waiting for his approval to enjoy what she had made.","The skipper thanked everyone who had helped keep the steamer afloat. The last passengers were enjoying roast chicken with rice. One child found a stray noodle in a borrowed serving bowl and asked whether the chef was testing him. The prize bottle went to Ess, who gave it to her uncle with permission from the organiser. She wanted the apprenticeship book donated by another cook instead. He offered a future lunch in exchange for a lesson. She had a sly smile when Cassian asked whether lessons were available to aristocratic beginners. \"You'll need a stronger relationship with vinegar,\" she said.","A record of the small donation appeared in Rowan’s credit-card account, a reminder that he had done more than tease. At dusk, Mara and Cassian stood alone by the stern. Superficially, her gown still looked immaculate. Flour coated one sleeve, and sauce had marked the hem. He drew a hand down her back and asked whether she regretted their sight-seeing afternoon.","She leaned into him. \"Only that I didn't photograph the hamburger.\"","\"Cruel woman.\"","\"Devoted woman,\" she corrected, and kissed him before he could argue."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1076,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0195\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵烧坏了午饭</div><h1>3. Long Live the Washing-Up King","volume":"婚后外传：最差国王与纸皇冠","anchor":"chapter-0196","paragraphs":["The hamlet chose Cassian as its king because he was tall enough to reach the bunting. Mara considered this a promising basis for monarchy. The crown was cardboard, the throne belonged to the kindergarten and the citizenry had armed itself with paper votes. He accepted his position with a solemnity that lasted until a small girl asked whether kings washed dishes.","\"This one does,\" Mara said. He began to scrub a plate, and the friendly crowd cheered.","The culture fair had been organised by a federation of local clubs. Their international visitors came from an education congress held nearby. A caravan of hired buses delivered them to the church, whose clergy had agreed to open the grounds. The Roman Catholic Church history display stood in the porch, beside a map showing the earlier settlement. A church volunteer warned the new king that even a cardboard dynasty must not block the entrance.","The core game was a staged constitutional crisis. His royal powers were unclear. The jury would grade his conduct, and a minority of children had voted against having a king at all. It would decide whether the king could keep his crown after a series of ridiculous challenges. Mae, a blind law school student, chaired it. Lio, a deaf tech instructor, ran the written cues with a sociable cruelty that delighted his friends. The event was not an elite examination; membership of the village league required curiosity and a modest fee, not a degree.","A well-run fair could survive opposing opinions. A chunk of the programme explained the difference between a council and a legislature. At this critical moment, a visitor wanted to call every assembly a parliament; another preferred congress. Mae told them to follow the fictional country printed on each game card. The same nation did not need every institution copied into it at once. A republic could have different arrangements from a monarchy. \"If you leave him unchallenged,\" Mara said, pointing at Cassian, \"he'll appoint himself supreme court too.\"","\"I would frown on that,\" Mae said.","Cassian looked wounded. \"You were asked to think of me as a king, not a menace.\"","The next challenge involved a souvenir company, Starling Corp, and three school stallholders pretending to form a cartel. They would all charge the same absurd price for a paper flower until customers found another supplier. A teacher would enforce the rules if the children became too persuasive. It was an elementary market game, not proof that sharing a picnic amounted to communism. A teacher with a lesson on collectivism was tired of that joke before the day began. She handed Cassian a sandwich and asked him to identify its owner. He was likely to be wrong about a second sandwich too. He guessed wrong. Mara ate it.","Beyond the gymnasium, a herd of sheep delayed the athletics parade. A flock of birds rose from the meadow as children gathered at a distance. The local fauna was not part of the staff, however useful it looked in photographs. A colony of swallows occupied the old eaves. The aviation club had therefore shifted its paper flight contest away from the nests. Its written explanation cited a National Academy of Sciences educational resource without pretending the academy had organised the fair.","The project was part of a sustainable development activity. A weather project used satellite data to compare the meadow with an older map. Mara needed one image to solve a clue. A blind guess would have missed the changed path. Lio's dedicated display could work off-line, so the crowded connection did not stop the game. Its so-called royal route led directly to the washing-up table. The council had designed this humiliation expressly for its temporary ruler.","Cassian attempted to rally support among the middle class visitors at the cake stall. They offered him a fork, not an uprising. In addition, a child offered him a crown sticker in exchange for a game token. Then she asked whether he would join the Labour Party. He said today's only party involved icing. Her grandmother, who had brought the old British campaign poster for a history booth, told him to stop dodging the question. The grandmother had spent years in the civil service and was difficult to impress.","Near her, a ministry display described ordinary public work. Its governance game asked who should be responsible for a broken bridge, then offered several different answers depending on the fictional country. A labour organiser challenged the unpaid tasks assigned to the king's attendants. The performers had a pay deal with the fair, and his jokes must not expand their hours. Cassian agreed immediately. Mara decided he might survive his reign after all.","The philharmonic students began a small ensemble by the gate. A science symposium had brought an astronomer who projected a galaxy inside a dark tent. She invited children to interact with the light pattern. A gang of friends swarmed toward it, while a literal swarm of bees in the distant hedge was left alone. The aggregation of so many activities was cheerful rather than tidy. Staff kept the walking route clear and collected litter as they went.","Rowan arrived with a mailing from the costume sponsor. The invitations had been widely shared. The fashion business had supplied a cape far too grand for the cardboard throne. An extra collar looked superfluous, so Cassian removed it. Its personnel would undoubtedly regret letting Mara choose the lining: it contained a hidden pocket for game tokens. She would redeem them at the final stall. A clumsy royal collector of service tax could not steal what he could not find.","Then the town crier announced that Cassian was wanted on the charge of stealing the ceremonial cake. The supposed cult of the perfect ruler had lasted forty minutes. Mara laughed so hard she had to hold his arm.","\"Undoubtedly a coup,\" he said.","\"A significant improvement in entertainment.\"","The jury sent him running before he could argue. The citizenry cheered. He crossed the church yard instead, pursued by three laughing children and his wife. The clergy watched without joining the chase. A king on the charge of stealing cake could request sanctuary, the volunteer said, but he would still have to answer the jury.","\"Friendly governance,\" he complained.","\"You chose the cape,\" Mara replied.","The caravan of buses now waited behind the church. The hamlet had become a small theatrical nation. The culture game was likely to make a patient man impatient. Its cardboard monarchy was supposed to fall by supper, though Cassian had not read that page. The citizenry wanted him captured in time for a parade. Mae, the blind law school student, had designed an alternative route with the council so nobody had to run to compete. Lio, the deaf tech instructor, kept the written clues updated and pointed slower visitors toward the next meeting point.","She wondered what grade her husband would receive for running away. A minority of guests had simply followed him to offer advice. Mara found her first token beneath a card about the legislature. To redeem it, she had to distinguish parliament from a village committee. The game's imaginary republic had chosen its own institutions. The next card described a congress; a child insisted that this was exactly the same as kindergarten because people sat in rows and argued over snacks. Mae said the comparison was not entirely superfluous. It was, however, incomplete.","In addition, the jury needed someone to scrub jam from the royal seal. Cassian had apparently tried to bribe an official with a sandwich. The paper flower cartel offered to conceal him for ten tokens. He refused. Mara, who had nine, offered a charming smile instead. The stallholders would not alienate their customers by changing the price for a duchess, and she applauded them for it. Starling Corp could learn from these school negotiators.","At the market corner, a labour organiser was explaining the performers' pay deal to a new volunteer. The fair would enforce its promised breaks even while a king was missing. A civil service retiree had taken over the costume desk so the paid attendant could eat. The old British Labour Party poster flapped above her, one corner held by a clip. A visiting minister's ministry had nothing to do with it; somebody had confused the poster's historical context with today's sponsorship.","The Roman Catholic Church display helped identify the fair's oldest building. A heritage league volunteer showed Mara a floor plan, while a federation steward cleared the entrance. Collectivism, the teacher remarked, could be discussed without pretending every useful act of cooperation was communism. She wanted to talk about a real concept, not spend the whole afternoon correcting a slogan. Mara promised to send her the king; he was excellent at being corrected.","Outside, the herd still occupied its meadow. The sheep had left the road, so the athletics club could rally for the children's race. A flock circled the old roof above the swallow colony. The fauna guide asked the aviation club to postpone another paper flight while the birds settled. The precautionary delay was short. A dedicated instructor found a different direction for the launch, rather than argue with a nest.","Mara stopped at the satellite data display to check her next clue. Even a cult of excellent rulers had to interact with ordinary map readers. The map named a path behind the astronomy tent. The written route was initially unclear, so Lio helped rectify the reversed arrow. His sociable grin accompanied a wicked new clue. Its source note still mentioned the National Academy of Sciences educational material used in class, clearly separate from the pupils' own work. In the tent, a galaxy turned overhead while the symposium astronomer answered questions. The international visitors had abandoned their congress badges and were sitting on the grass like everyone else.","Then Mara spotted the cape between two tents. Cassian was helping a lost boy find his aunt. She slowed down. No trophy required dragging him away before the child felt safe. He saw her and made an appeal with his eyebrows so ridiculous that she almost gave them away by laughing. She let him finish, then blocked the narrow royal route with one hand against a pole.","\"Caught,\" she said.","\"I may be in need of favourable treatment.\"","\"The fashion business provided a hidden pocket. Perhaps you should have used it.\"","He placed the folded corner of a cake napkin in her hand. The printed clue contained the word CORE. Beneath it was a sketch of the civic map. Mara recognised that the final place would be in the centre, not at the so-called palace. She would think of the king as a clue, a strategy with considerable marital potential.","Cassian tried to distract her with a kiss. It worked, but only briefly. She led him back toward the jury with their fingers linked. His clumsy attempt to hide the napkin in his sleeve had already been widely witnessed. A gang of children followed, convinced they were observing the end of a dynasty. The elite visitors from the education congress cheered as loudly as the middle class parents at the cake table.","The tech display stayed available off-line. The fair's personnel collected litter and prepared the final stage. A mailing from the sponsor announced more costume vouchers; guests could ignore it without losing their place. The membership desk stayed open to late arrivals. A swarm of visitors wanted to see whether the king could prevail. The aggregation would have blocked the path if Lio had not moved two chairs.","\"Undoubtedly guilty,\" Rowan said.","Mae warned him not to pre-empt a supreme court he had not been invited to form. A Very Short Dynasty","volume":"婚后外传：最差国王与纸皇冠","anchor":"chapter-0198","paragraphs":["The king stood before the jury on the charge of stealing cake. His wife was invited to defend him. Mara asked whether this was strictly necessary, since she was the person who had caught him. Mae, the blind law school student, said the rule had been printed before they knew who would play. A well-run trial would not alienate its own witnesses. A temporary monarchy could contain a procedural flaw without becoming a tragedy.","Cassian put his hand over his heart. \"Think of me as a victim.\"","\"That may be the most difficult clue today,\" Mara said.","A precautionary extra chair marked the meeting point. Lio, the deaf tech instructor, projected the charges in large text. The first photograph showed Cassian carrying an empty plate. The second showed a child eating cake nearby. Neither established theft. The citizenry booed this disappointing discovery, then cheered when a third photograph showed Rowan licking icing from his thumb. The hamlet's most sociable sponsor had framed its king to make the final game more entertaining.","The council declared the evidence insufficient. A minority had voted for conviction, chiefly because they wanted another chase. The final grade for his reign remained disputed. Its cardboard king could redeem his reputation by sharing the remaining cake. Rowan surrendered a chunk with exaggerated reluctance. The core lesson, Mae said, was that a jury must not decide from a dramatic cape. A supreme court might have larger problems to address, but children could practise asking what a picture actually showed.","The education congress visitors applauded the game. Its international organiser wanted to adapt it for another fair, keeping the imaginary nation clearly distinct from any real country. A legislature could take different forms. Parliament and congress belonged in the discussion, not as interchangeable decorations. The civil service retiree offered the handwritten notes that had clarified the ministry challenge. Mara wondered whether Cassian had expected to acquire homework with his crown.","The teacher with the collectivism activity finally sat down. The discussion of communism had survived without becoming a quarrel about who owned a picnic table. Her paper flower cartel had dissolved when a child opened a competing stall. Starling Corp donated the unsold flowers to the kindergarten. Its fashion business representative preferred this ending to watching Mara convert the cape into a prize for herself. The personnel had worked hard, and the pay deal gave them a proper finishing time.","A labour organiser rang the closing bell. Beside her, the Labour Party history poster was taken down carefully, its British context labelled before storage. The Roman Catholic Church material returned to the church porch with the other heritage displays. Clergy helped fold tables rather than stand apart from the ordinary work. A league volunteer carried the last box to the gymnasium, while the federation coordinator checked that nobody's transport had been forgotten.","Cassian tried to scrub icing from a royal cuff. Mara took the cloth and dabbed the last mark herself. \"A friendly coup,\" she said.","\"I shall frown on elected office thereafter.\"","\"You weren't elected. You reached the bunting.\"","\"An ability the middle class may yet appreciate.\"","A small girl offered him her paper vote as a souvenir. She had chosen a republic because it meant he could go home after supper. Mara approved the argument. The new governance arrangement required no dynasty, and the local elite could carry its own chairs. The so-called palace became a reading corner again, its monarchy packed into a cardboard box beside a dragon mask.","Outside, a herd of sheep moved toward the evening enclosure. A flock of swallows passed above their colony in the eaves. The fauna guide stopped two guests from approaching the nesting wall for a better photograph. Sustainable development, a symposium speaker had said, could begin with leaving a place fit for the things that lived there. The aviation club's last flight therefore took place in the empty field, where paper wings would not disturb a real one.","Mara watched the small plane rise, then fall. The boy who had made it ran after it without looking disappointed. In the tent, the astronomer switched off the galaxy projection. Her National Academy of Sciences reference book went back into its protective cover. The satellite data display would remain available at school; students had asked to continue the project. The science was not superfluous because the spectacle had finished. It had begun an interest beyond the fair.","The hamlet was almost quiet again. The caravan waited beyond the field. The last parade was a short procession to the buses. The philharmonic players carried their instruments, and the gymnasium caretaker led with a borrowed drum. An aggregation of tired guests became, briefly, a gang of terrible singers. A literal swarm of bees remained by the hedge, entirely unimpressed. A committee member gathered the final litter from the path. Mara picked up a dropped membership card and handed it to a man who had spent half the afternoon searching for it.","Rowan could rally them for one group photograph, but no more speeches. His mailing list would receive a summary later. Rowan promised to rectify the mistaken caption on one photograph. It was a small but significant correction: Mara had caught the king, not rescued him. The fair had undoubtedly been a success, though not every activity worked uniformly well. Its caption would describe culture as something people made together, not a cult of important patrons. One donor had asked for a private throne picture. The child who owned the chair said no, and the donor survived.","Cassian and Mara walked toward the car after the buses had gone. \"Do I prevail?\" he asked.","\"The jury remains divided.\"","\"A disturbing foundation for romance.\"","She pulled the cardboard crown from his pocket and put it on his head. Her kiss was slow enough to make him forget his next complaint.
